<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 14pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 16pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=center><B>PART I</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
1. &nbsp;</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 83px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Business</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>General</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Florida
Public Utilities Company (FPU) was incorporated on March 6, 1924 and
reincorporated on April 29, 1925 under the 1925 Florida Corporation Law. We
provide natural gas, electricity and propane gas to residential, commercial and
industrial customers in Florida. We do not produce energy and are not a
generating utility. Our regulated segments sell natural gas and electricity to
approximately 83,000 customers and our unregulated segment sells propane gas
through a wholly owned subsidiary, Flo-Gas Corporation, to approximately 13,000
customers. We also sell merchandise and other service-related products on a
limited basis as a complement to the natural and propane gas segments.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
three primary business segments are aligned with our products and are natural
gas, electric and propane gas. &nbsp;The Florida Public Service Commission
(FPSC) regulates the natural gas and electric segments. We operate through four
divisions based on geographic areas:</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(1)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">South
Florida Division - provides natural and propane gas to customers in West Palm
Beach, Palm Beach Gardens, North Palm Beach, Jupiter, Riviera Beach, Palm Beach,
Lake Worth, Royal Palm Beach, Wellington, Boynton Beach, Delray Beach, Boca
Raton, Lauderdale Lakes, Deerfield Beach, Stuart, Palm City and other areas near
these cities.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(2)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Central
Florida Division - provides natural and propane gas to customers in Sanford,
Deland, Deltona, DeBary, Orange City, Lake Mary, Winter Springs, New Smyrna
Beach, Edgewater, Longwood, Port Orange and other areas near these cities.
&nbsp;Our previous West Florida Division, which provides propane gas to
customers in Dunnellon, Inglis, Crystal River, Inverness, Brooksville and other
areas near these cities, has been consolidated with our Central Florida
Division.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(3)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Northwest
Florida Division - provides electricity to customers in Marianna, Bristol,
Altha, Cottondale, Malone, Alford and other areas near these cities.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(4)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Northeast
Florida Division - provides electricity and propane gas to customers in
Fernandina Beach, Jacksonville, Callahan, Yulee and other areas near these
cities.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Business
Environment</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
historic growth that has fueled strong demand for natural and propane gas over
the last decade has slowed with the slowdown in the new construction housing
market and the economy in general. However, interest is growing among those who
wish to use natural and propane gas as an environmentally friendly, alternative
energy source that is a very reliable source of energy in the event of a power
outage. During 2007, the price of natural gas remained stable as no adverse
conditions existed to effect pricing. The cost of propane gas increased
primarily in response to the unprecedented increase in the price of crude
oil.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Historically,
our sales in the electric segment have not been impacted by increasing electric
costs due to long-term favorable fixed price contracts for purchasing
electricity. However, our long-term contracts concluded at the end of 2006 for
our Northeast division and at the end of 2007 for our Northwest division. We now
have new contracts in place with pricing much closer to current market prices.
Our electric selling prices, as a result of these increased fuel costs,
significantly increased. Although this does not directly impact our income from
operations as increased fuel costs are passed through to the customer, this may
decrease the volume of electricity sold, thereby decreasing income from
operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Business
Segments</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are organized in three operating and reporting segments: natural gas, electric
and propane gas. We are also involved in limited merchandise sales and other
services in our natural gas and propane gas areas to complement these segments.
For information concerning revenues, operating income and identifiable assets of
each of our segments, see Note 13 in Notes to Consolidated Financial
Statements.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>Natural Gas</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Natural
gas is primarily composed of&nbsp;methane, which is a colorless, odorless fuel
that burns cleaner than many other traditional fossil fuels. &nbsp;Odorant is
added to enable easy detection of a gas leak.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
provide natural gas to customers in our South and Central Florida divisions. The
vast majority of the natural gas we distribute is purchased in the Gulf Coast
region, both onshore and offshore.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
use Florida Gas Transmission (FGT) to transport our natural gas supplies through
their pipeline into peninsular Florida. FGT is under the jurisdiction of the
Federal Energy Regulatory Commission (FERC). &nbsp;We use gas marketers and
producers to procure all gas supplies for our markets. We use Florida City Gas,
Indiantown Gas Company and TECO Peoples Gas to provide wholesale gas sales
services in areas distant from our interconnections with FGT. We pass all fuel
costs on to our customers. &nbsp;We also transport natural gas for customers who
purchase their own gas supplies and arrange for pipeline transportation.&nbsp;
Our operating results are not adversely affected if our customers purchase gas
from third parties because we do not profit on the fuel portion of sales.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
natural gas revenues are affected by the rates charged to customers, supply
costs for natural gas, economic conditions in our service areas and weather.
Although the FPSC permits us to pass through to customers the increase in price
for our gas costs, higher rates may cause customers to purchase less natural
gas.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
current portfolio of natural gas customers is reasonably diverse, with the
largest customer using natural gas for the generation of electricity.&nbsp; We
are not dependent on any single natural gas customer for over ten percent of our
total natural gas revenues.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Electric</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
provide electricity to our customers in our Northwest and Northeast Florida
divisions.&nbsp; Wholesale electricity is purchased from two suppliers: Gulf
Power Company and JEA (formerly Jacksonville Electric Authority). &nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">During
2006 we completed negotiations with JEA and executed final contracts for the
supply of electricity in our Northeast division beginning January 1, 2007 and
our Northwest division from Gulf Power Company beginning in January 1, 2008. The
rates charged to our customers significantly increased when the new contracts
became effective in 2007 and 2008 because the prices are closer to market price.
We are unable to estimate what impact higher rates could have on electric
consumption in the future, but electricity usage could decrease.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Northwest and Northeast divisions&nbsp;experience a variety of weather
patterns.&nbsp; Hot summers and cold winters produce year-round electric sales
that normally do not have highly seasonal fluctuations. &nbsp;None of the
electric segment&#146;s customers represent more than ten percent of our total
electric revenues.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
electric utility industry has not been deregulated in the state of
Florida.&nbsp; All customers within a given service or franchise area purchase
from a single electricity provider in that area.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Propane
Gas</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
provide propane gas to customers in our Northeast, Central and South Florida
divisions and can purchase our propane gas supply from several different
wholesale companies. Propane gas supply into Florida comes from a diverse
assortment of delivery methods such as waterborne barge transports that deliver
to port terminals in Tampa and Ft. Lauderdale, and the Dixie Pipeline. Railcar
and tractor trailer transport the gas to our storage facilities. &nbsp;We
believe that the propane gas supply infrastructure is adequate to meet the needs
of the industry in Florida.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Strategy</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
strategy is to leverage our expertise in the natural gas, electric and propane
gas distribution business to assist us in consistently meeting our customer&#146;s
expectations. Our core focus is to build mutually beneficial relationships with
builders, developers and customers with high-energy usage requirements. Included
in our strategy is a plan to enhance our future success by expanding our service
territory into new areas with high growth potential.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Competition</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
do not face substantial competition in our electric divisions. &nbsp;This is
because no other competitor can currently provide electricity in our areas due
to FPSC regulations and territorial agreements between utilities. In addition,
natural gas as an alternative fuel is only available in a small area in our
electric divisions. Although our natural gas segment operates with the same
types of guidelines, there is competition from electric utilities. Normally each
home will have electricity as a base fuel and natural gas as an alternative
source of energy used for cooking and heating. Electricity competes with natural
gas, in large part based on the cost of fuel. Our propane gas segment is
unregulated and faces competition from other suppliers of propane gas as well as
electricity and alternative energy sources. Competition in the propane gas
segment is primarily based on price and service.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Rates
and Regulation</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
natural gas and electric segments are highly regulated by the FPSC. &nbsp;The
FPSC has the authority to regulate our rates, conditions of service, issuance of
securities and certain other matters affecting our natural gas and electric
operations. &nbsp;As a result, FPSC regulation has a significant effect on our
results of operations. &nbsp;The FPSC approves rates that are intended to permit
a specified rate of return on investment and recovery of prudent expenses.
&nbsp;Our rate tariffs allow the cost of natural gas and electricity to be
passed through to customers. &nbsp;Increases in the operating expenses of the
regulated segments may require us to request increases in the rates charged to
our customers. &nbsp;The FPSC has granted us the flexibility of automatically
passing on increased expenses for certain fuel costs to customers. &nbsp;Other
operational expenses, such as pension and medical expenses require us to
petition the FPSC for rate increases. &nbsp;The FPSC is likely to grant rate
increases to offset increased expenditures necessary for business operations.
&nbsp;We successfully petitioned for an electric rate increase on March 17, 2004
and a natural gas rate increase on November 18, 2004. &nbsp;We are currently
seeking additional electric rate relief in 2008 to recover increased costs
including the recent storm preparedness requirements, which were mandated to
improve reliability of electric utility systems.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are subject to federal and state regulation with respect to soil, groundwater,
employee health and safety matters and to environmental regulations issued by
the Florida Department of Environmental Protection (FDEP), the United States
Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) and other federal and state agencies.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Prior
to the widespread availability of natural gas, we manufactured gas for sale to
our customers. We have also purchased land from companies that at one time
manufactured gas. The process for manufacturing gas produced by-products and
residuals such as coal tar. The remnants of these residuals are sometimes found
at former gas manufacturing sites. These sites face environmental regulation
from various agencies including the FDEP and EPA on necessary cleanup and
restoration. For information on our environmentally impacted sites, please see
Item 3, Legal Proceedings.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Franchises</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
hold franchises in each of the incorporated municipalities that require
franchise agreements in order to provide natural gas and electricity.
&nbsp;Generally, these franchises have terms ranging from 10 to 30 years and
terminate on varying dates. We are currently in negotiations for franchises with
certain municipalities for new service areas along with renewing some existing
franchises. We continue to provide services to these municipalities and do not
anticipate any interruption in our service.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 28px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Employees</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">As
of February 1, 2008, we employed 356 employees, including 10 part-time and 5
temporary employees. Of these employees, 174 were covered under union contracts
with two labor unions, the Internal Brotherhood of Electric Workers and the
International Chemical Workers Union. We believe that our labor relations with
employees are good.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 28px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Available
Information</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
file periodic reports including our Form 10-Qs, Form 10-Ks and Form 8-Ks with
the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC). Copies of recent SEC filings as
well as our Code of Ethics Policy can be obtained through our website (<FONT
color=#0000ff><U>http://www.fpuc.com</U></FONT>).</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
1A.</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 83px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Risk
Factors </B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>A
substantial portion of our revenues and, to a large extent, our profitability,
depends upon rates determined by the FPSC.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">FPSC
regulates many aspects of our natural gas and electric operating segments,
including the retail rates that we may charge customers for natural gas and
electric service. &nbsp;Our retail rates are set by the FPSC using a
cost-of-service approach that takes into account our historical operating
expenses, our fixed obligations and recovery of our capital investments,
including potentially stranded obligations. Using this approach, the FPSC sets
rates at a level calculated to recover such costs, adjusted to reflect known and
measurable changes, plus a permitted return on investment. &nbsp;Any rate
adjustments to recover increased costs or to otherwise improve our profitability
must be obtained through a petition, or rate case, filed with the FPSC.
&nbsp;The rates permitted by the FPSC will determine a substantial portion of
our revenues and may have a material impact on our consolidated earnings, cash
flows and financial position, as well as our ability to maintain our common
stock dividend or to increase our dividends in the future.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Some
of our natural gas and electric service costs may not be fully recovered through
retail rates.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P><A name=HIT1></A>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
natural gas and electric service retail rates, once established by the FPSC,
remain fixed until changed in a subsequent rate case. &nbsp;We may at any time
elect to file a rate case to request a change in our rates or intervening
parties may request that the FPSC review our rates for possible adjustment,
subject to any limitations that may have been ordered by the FPSC. Earnings
could be reduced if our operating costs increase more than our revenues during
the period between rate cases. &nbsp;In addition, even if we decide to file a
rate case, our request for a rate adjustment may be rejected. &nbsp;Third
parties to a rate case or the FPSC staff may contend that our current rates are
excessive and petition for a decrease in rates. A petition for rate increase by
us could be denied on that or another basis.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Our
business segments are sensitive to variations in weather.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
segments are affected by variations in general weather conditions and unusually
severe weather. We forecast energy sales on the basis of normal weather and on
historical average. Significant variations from normal weather could have a
material impact on energy sales. Unusual weather, such as hurricanes, could also
adversely affect operating costs and sales.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
natural gas and propane gas customers use gas primarily for heating purposes.
&nbsp;As a result, our natural gas and propane gas sales peak in the winter and
are more weather sensitive than electricity sales, which can peak in both summer
and winter periods. Mild winter weather in Florida can be expected to negatively
impact results from our natural gas, electric and propane gas operations. Severe
weather conditions could also interrupt or slow down service and increase the
operating costs of any of our segments.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>We
operate in an increasingly competitive industry, which may affect our future
earnings.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-RIGHT: 1px; PADDING-LEFT: 78px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: -48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Natural
Gas</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
natural gas distribution industry has been subject to competitive forces for
several years. We receive&nbsp;our supply of natural gas at thirteen city gate
stations connected to an&nbsp;interstate pipeline system owned by FGT, one gate
station connected to an intrastate pipeline owned by Florida City Gas Company,
one meter connected to the Indiantown Gas Company distribution system in
Indiantown, Florida, and one meter connected to the TECO Peoples Gas
distribution system in Ocala, Florida.&nbsp; Gulfstream Natural Gas System
currently serves peninsular Florida with interstate natural gas transmission
service; however, we cannot predict if this system will be extended to other
areas near our existing facilities and how it could affect our natural gas
operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-RIGHT: 1px; PADDING-LEFT: 78px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: -48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Electric</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
U.S. electric power industry has been undergoing restructuring in many areas.
There is competition in wholesale power sales on a national level. Some states
have mandated or encouraged competition at the retail level. While there is
active wholesale competition in Florida, the retail electric business has
remained substantially free from direct competition. Changes in the competitive
environment caused by legislation, regulation, market conditions or initiatives
of other electric power providers, particularly with respect to retail
competition, could adversely affect our financial condition and results of
operations. &nbsp;To the extent competitive pressures increase and the pricing
and sale of electricity assumes more of the characteristics of a commodity
business, the economics of our electric operating segment could change. In
addition, regulatory changes may increase access to electricity transmission
grids by utility and non-utility purchasers and sellers of electricity, thus
potentially resulting in a significant number of additional competitors.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-RIGHT: 1px; PADDING-LEFT: 78px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: -48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Propane
Gas</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
propane gas business is our only non-regulated business segment. &nbsp;Because
it is not regulated, we face significant competition. &nbsp;Our propane gas
business competes directly with other distributors of propane gas, and other
sources of energy including natural gas and electric. &nbsp;If we cannot compete
effectively in the propane gas business, whether on the basis of price, customer
service, alternative energy sources or otherwise, it would have a material
adverse effect on our financial condition and results of operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Our
business could be adversely affected if our supply of natural gas is
interrupted.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">FGT&#146;s
pipeline system transports all of our natural gas. &nbsp;FGT is owned by Citrus
Corporation, which is jointly owned by Cross Country Energy Corporation and El
Paso Corporation. Our ability to receive a normal supply of natural gas could
adversely affect earnings if there is an interruption in FGT&#146;s service.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>General
economic conditions may adversely affect our segments.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
segments are affected by general economic conditions. The consumption of the
energy we supply is directly tied to the economy. A downturn in the economy in
our local areas of operations, as well as on the state, national and
international levels, could adversely affect the performance of our segments.
&nbsp;Changes in political climate, including terrorist activities, could
further negatively impact our performance. If tourism is down, then the demand
for the energy we supply is reduced.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Commodity
price changes may affect the operating costs and competitive position of our
segments.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
segments are sensitive to changes in coal, gas, oil and other commodity prices.
If we are unable to increase the rates we charge to customers to reflect
increases in these commodity prices, our margins and earnings will be lowered.
&nbsp;If increased prices for any of these commodities persist for substantial
periods, our competitive position could be adversely affected by customers who
switch to cheaper energy sources. Further, natural gas prices have been
increasingly volatile and, accordingly, the earnings from our natural gas
operations are increasingly difficult to predict. Recent increases in oil prices
may have an adverse impact on the Company&#146;s earnings.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>We
could incur material expenses as a result of our obligations to comply with
existing and new environmental laws and regulations.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are subject to environmental regulations in connection with the ongoing conduct
of our business and to civil and criminal liability for failure to comply with
these regulations. In addition, new environmental laws and regulations, or new
interpretations of existing laws and regulations, affecting our operations or
facilities may be adopted which may cause us to incur additional expenses.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are subject to federal and state legislation with respect to soil, groundwater,
employee health and safety matters and to environmental regulations issued by
the FDEP, the EPA and other federal and state agencies. &nbsp;We may incur
material future expenditures in order to comply with the environmental laws and
regulations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>We
rely on a limited number of natural gas and electric suppliers, the loss of
which could materially adversely affect our financial condition and results of
operations.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Two
pipeline suppliers under several contracts expiring at various dates through
2023, transport our natural gas to us. &nbsp;These contracts have provisions
which allow us to extend the terms ranging from 2020 to 2032. &nbsp;Our electric
services are provided by two suppliers under contracts which expire in 2017. If
we were to lose any of these contracts we might not be able to replace the
corresponding energy source on acceptable terms, if at all. &nbsp;In addition,
in the event of the expiration of the contracts, we might not be able to renew
them on favorable terms, if at all. &nbsp;As a result, the loss of any of these
suppliers, the termination of any of these supply contracts, or the non-renewal
of any of these supply contracts before or upon their expiration could have
material adverse effects on our financial condition and results of
operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>New
supply contracts could result in substantial increases to our prices, and could
materially adversely affect our financial condition and results of
operations.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
have two pipeline suppliers for natural gas and two electric suppliers under
contract for supply through various dates in the future. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
recent renewal of the electricity supply contracts resulted in the cost of
electricity increasing significantly over historic prices.&nbsp;&nbsp;Extensions
or renewals&nbsp;of&nbsp;our natural gas contracts could&nbsp;result in the cost
of natural gas increasing.&nbsp;&nbsp;Although these increases are currently
passed through to our customers, they could have a significant impact on our
customers&#146; usage. If recovery of fuel costs was denied by the FPSC in the
future, it would have a significant impact on our earnings and our financial
condition.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Fluctuation
in prices under long-term purchase and transportation commitments may have an
adverse effect on our financial condition and results of operations.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">To
ensure a reliable supply of electricity and natural gas at competitive prices,
we have entered into purchase and transportation contracts with various
suppliers and producers, which expire at various dates through 2023. Purchase
prices under these contracts are determined by formulas either based on market
prices or at fixed prices.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">As
of December 31, 2007, we have firm purchase and transportation commitments
adequate to supply our expected sales requirements for electricity with
contracts that will expire in 2017. Our contract in the Northeast division of
the electric segment began January 1, 2007 and expires on December 31, 2017, and
our contract with a supplier for the Northwest division began January 1, 2008
and expires on December 31, 2017. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
natural gas pipeline transportation contracts expire in parts in 2010, 2015 and
2023. We are committed to pay demand or similar fixed charges monthly through
2023 related to the natural gas pipeline transportation
agreements.&nbsp;Significant fluctuation in prices under these long-term
purchase and transportation commitments may have a material adverse effect on
our financial condition and results of operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Problems
with operations could materially adversely impact us.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are subject to various operational risks, including accidents, outages,
equipment breakdowns or failures, or operations below expected levels of
performance or efficiency. Problems such as the breakdown or failure of
transmission lines, pipelines or other equipment or processes and interruptions
in service which would result in performance below affected levels of output or
efficiency, particularly if extending for prolonged periods of time, would have
a material adverse effect on our financial condition and results of
operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>We
are vulnerable to interest rate changes and may not have access to capital at
favorable rates, if at all.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Changes
in interest rates can affect our cost of borrowing on our line of credit, on
refinancing of debt maturities and on incremental borrowing to fund new
investments. Because our stock is not widely held and has a low trading volume,
we may not be able to access the equity market or may be limited in the amount
of equity financing. If we are unable to obtain equity or debt financing on
satisfactory terms, our ability to fund capital expenditures and other
commitments will be impaired. Moreover, even if available, the capital may not
be available on favorable terms and the cost of such financing could reduce our
margins and materially adversely affect our results of operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Failure
to effectively and efficiently manage our growth, as well as changes in our
business strategies, could have a negative impact on our performance.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">An
essential part of our business strategy is to grow our businesses. Much of our
growth depends on our ability to find attractive development opportunities and
to obtain the necessary financing for them. Our outlook is based on our
expectation that we will be successful in finding and capitalizing on
development opportunities, but our efforts may not be successful. Our failure to
effectively and efficiently manage our growth, as well as changes in our
business strategies, may have a material adverse effect on our financial
condition and results of operations. If we grow our business with acquisitions
there is a risk the acquisition will not have a positive effect on our financial
condition.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Our
ability to pay dividends on our common stock is limited.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
cannot guarantee that we will continue to pay dividends at our current annual
dividend rate or at all. &nbsp;In particular, our ability to pay or increase
dividends in the future will depend upon, among other things, our future
earnings, our cash requirements and our debt covenants. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Provisions
in our certificate of reincorporation, certain agreements, and the Florida
Business Corporation Act may inhibit a takeover, which could adversely affect
the value of our common stock.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
certificate of reincorporation as well as provisions of the Florida Business
Corporation Act (FBCA), contain provisions that could delay or prevent a change
of control in our management that shareholders might consider favorable and may
prevent them from receiving a takeover premium for their shares.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
certificate of reincorporation contains provisions that make it more difficult
to obtain control of our company through transactions which have not received
the approval of our board of directors. &nbsp;These provisions include
supermajority voting requirements for certain transactions with affiliated
persons, staggering the terms of the members of our board of directors, and
certain procedural requirements relating to shareholder meetings and amendments
to our certificate of reincorporation or bylaws.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
addition, Florida has enacted legislation that may deter or frustrate takeovers
of Florida corporations. &nbsp;Subject to certain exceptions, the "Control Share
Acquisitions" section of the FBCA generally provides that shares acquired in
excess of certain specified thresholds, beginning at 20% of a corporation&#146;s
outstanding voting shares, will not possess any voting rights unless such voting
rights are approved by a majority vote of the corporation&#146;s disinterested
shareholders.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
"Affiliated Transactions" section of the FBCA generally requires majority
approval by disinterested directors or supermajority approval by disinterested
shareholders of certain specified transactions (such as mergers, consolidations,
sales of assets, issuance or transfer of shares or reclassifications of
securities) between a corporation and a holder of more than 10% of the
outstanding shares of the corporation, or any affiliate of such shareholder.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
have agreements with our three executive officers that provide for significant
payments to those executives upon a change in control under certain
circumstances. The existence of these contracts may make an acquisition of our
company less attractive to a possible buyer. &nbsp;Finally, our pension plan
contains provisions that upon a change in control an acquirer will be limited in
changing the pension plan for a period of time without incurring additional
pension expenses for enhanced benefits to pension participants.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Conflict
or turmoil in oil producing countries could impact future prices for commodities
including natural gas, propane gas and electricity, and increases in these
prices could materially affect our financial condition and results of
operations.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Worldwide
turmoil could cause the cost of crude oil and its associated products to rise on
concerns of the conflicts interfering with the production of crude oil. If these
conflicts are large, escalate or spread, the increase to the cost of all
fuel-related commodities could be substantial. These increases could materially
adversely affect our financial condition and results of operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>If
conservation costs incurred are determined not to be appropriate for recovery
through conservation programs and rates, these costs would directly impact our
net operating income and could significantly decrease our earnings.</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company participates in energy conservation programs to provide incentives to
customers to conserve energy. Costs for these programs are passed directly
through to our regulated customers and are recovered through conservation rates.
&nbsp;These programs and costs are reviewed and approved by the Florida Public
Service Commission on an annual basis. There can be no assurance the FPSC will
continue to approve our recovery of those costs and this could have an adverse
effect on our operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
1B.</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 83px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Unresolved
Staff Comments</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 28px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">None</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
2.</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 83px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Properties</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
have natural gas, electric and propane gas related properties. These properties
include transmission, distribution, storage and general facilities at various
locations in our service areas. We do not have generating facilities. We
maintain property that is adequate for our current operations and we expand our
existing facilities as required by growth or other operational needs.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
own natural gas mains that distribute gas through 1,611 miles of pipe located in
Central and South Florida. Additionally, we have adequate gate stations to
handle receipt of the gas in each distribution system.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
the electric segment, we own 22 miles of electric transmission lines located in
Northeast Florida and 1,092 miles of electric distribution lines located in
Northeast and Northwest Florida. The distribution lines are installed both under
and above ground and many of the coastal locations have under ground facilities.
All transmission lines are installed above ground. Additionally, we own various
substations and regulator stations that are used in our electric operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
propane gas segment has bulk storage facilities and tank installations on
customers' premises. We also have 16 community gas systems that distribute
propane gas to customers in specific developments. These systems are subject to
the Federal Department of Transportation Office of Pipeline Safety
Regulations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
own office and warehouse facilities in Northwest, Northeast, Central, and South
Florida, which are used for our operations and storage of materials. We also
have various easements and other assets located throughout our service areas
that are utilized by our operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
own a three-story building in West Palm Beach where our corporate headquarters
are located.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">All
of our property is subject to a lien collateralizing our funded indebtedness
under our Mortgage Indenture as discussed in Note 1-J in Notes to Consolidated
Financial Statements.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
3. &nbsp;&nbsp;Legal Proceedings </B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
use or have used several properties with contamination that have pending or
threatened environmental litigation. We are in the process of investigating and
assessing this litigation. &nbsp;We intend to vigorously defend our rights in
this litigation. &nbsp;We have insurance and rate relief to cover losses or
expenses incurred as a result of this litigation. &nbsp;We believe all future
contamination assessment and remedial costs, legal fees and other related
expenses would not exceed the combined sum of any insurance proceeds received
and any rate relief granted.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>West
Palm Beach Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company is currently evaluating remedial options to respond to environmental
impacts to soil and groundwater at and in the immediate vicinity of a parcel of
property owned by it in West Palm Beach, Florida upon which the Company
previously operated a gasification plant. The Company entered into a Consent
Order with the FDEP effective April 8, 1991, that requires the Company to
delineate the extent of soil and groundwater impacts associated with the prior
operation of the gasification plant and to remediate such soil and groundwater
impacts, if necessary. The Company completed field investigations for the
contamination assessment task in October 2006.&nbsp; Thereafter, the Company
retained an engineering consultant, the RETEC Group, Inc. (RETEC), to perform a
feasibility study to evaluate appropriate remedies for the site to respond to
the reported soil and groundwater impacts.&nbsp; On&nbsp;November 30,
2006,&nbsp;RETEC transmitted a feasibility study to the Company and FDEP.&nbsp;
The feasibility study evaluated a wide range of remedial alternatives.&nbsp;The
total costs for the remedies evaluated in the feasibility study ranged from a
low of $2.8 million to a high of $54.6 million.&nbsp; Based on the likely
acceptability of proven remedial technologies described in the feasibility study
and implemented at similar sites, consulting/remediation costs are projected to
range from $4.6 million to $17.9 million. This range of costs covers such
remedies as in situ solidification for the deeper impacts,&nbsp;excavation of
surficial soils,&nbsp;installation of a barrier wall with a permeable
biotreatment zone, or some combination of these remedies.&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">By
letter dated May 7, 2007, FDEP provided its comments to the&nbsp;feasibility
study, the substance of which was discussed at a meeting between the Company and
FDEP on September 14, 2007.&nbsp;&nbsp;A response to&nbsp;the comments was
submitted by the Company to FDEP on October 31, 2007.&nbsp; We are currently
awaiting FDEP's comments to the response.&nbsp; </P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Based
on the&nbsp;information provided in the feasibility study,&nbsp;the remaining
legal fees are currently projected to be&nbsp;approximately $295,000. Consulting
and remediation costs are projected to range from $4.6 million to $17.9
million.&nbsp;Thus, the Company's total probable legal and cleanup
costs&nbsp;for the West Palm Beach site are&nbsp;currently projected&nbsp;to
range&nbsp;from $4.9 million to $18.2 million. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Sanford
Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company owns a parcel of property located in Sanford, Florida, upon which a
gasification plant was operated prior to the Company&#146;s acquisition of the
property. Following discovery of soil and groundwater impacts on the property,
the Company has participated with four former owners and operators of the
gasification plant in the funding of numerous investigations of the extent of
the impacts and the identification of an appropriate remedy. On or about March
25, 1998, the Company executed an Administrative Order on Consent (AOC) with the
four former owners and operators (collectively, the Group) and the EPA. This AOC
obligated the Group to implement a Remedial Investigation/Feasibility Study
(RI/FS) and to pay EPA's past and future oversight costs. The Group also entered
into a Participation Agreement and an Escrow Agreement on or about April 13,
1998 (WFS Participation Agreement). Work under the</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">RI/FS
AOC and RI/FS Participation Agreement is now complete and the Company has no
further obligations under either agreement.</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In&nbsp;late
September&nbsp;2006, EPA&nbsp;sent a Special Notice Letter to&nbsp;the Company,
notifying it, and the other responsible parties at the site (Florida Power
Corporation, Florida Power &amp; Light Company, Atlanta Gas Light Company, and
the City of Sanford, Florida, collectively with FPUC, "the Sanford&nbsp;Group"),
of EPA's selection of a final remedy for OU1 (soils), OU2 (groundwater), and OU3
(sediments) for the site.&nbsp; The&nbsp;total estimated remediation costs for
the Sanford gasification plant site are now projected to be $12.9
million.&nbsp;The Sanford Group was further advised that EPA was willing to
negotiate a consent decree with the Sanford Group to provide for the
implementation of the&nbsp;final remedy approved by EPA for the site.&nbsp; </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
January 2007, the Company&nbsp;and other members of the Sanford Group signed a
Third Participation Agreement, which provides for funding the&nbsp;final remedy
approved by EPA for the site. &nbsp;The Company's share of remediation
costs&nbsp;under the Third Participation Agreement is set at a maximum of
$650,000, providing the total cost of the&nbsp;final remedy&nbsp;does not exceed
$13 million.&nbsp; At present, it is not anticipated that the total cost will
exceed $13 million.&nbsp; If it does, the Sanford Group&nbsp;members have agreed
to negotiate in good faith at such time that it appears that the total cost will
exceed $13 million for the allocation of the additional cost.&nbsp;&nbsp;The
Company&nbsp;has advised the other members of the Sanford Group that the Company
is unwilling at this time to agree to pay any sum in excess of the $650,000
committed by the Company in the Third Participation Agreement.</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">On
June 26, 2007, the Sanford Group transmitted to EPA a Consent Decree signed by
all Group Members, providing for the implementation by the Sanford Group of the
remedy selected by EPA&nbsp;for the&nbsp;site.&nbsp;&nbsp;The consent decree is
currently being circulated within EPA and the United States Department of
Justice for execution by those parties.&nbsp; Thereafter, the consent decree
will be lodged with the federal court in Orlando, Florida.&nbsp; Following a
public comment period, it is anticipated that the federal court will enter the
consent decree.&nbsp; The Sanford Group will then be obligated to implement the
remedy approved by EPA for the&nbsp;site.&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Remaining
legal fees/costs are&nbsp;currently projected to be&nbsp;approximately
$77,000.&nbsp;The Company's obligation under the Third Participation Agreement
is $650,000. Thus, the Company's total probable legal and cleanup costs&nbsp;for
the Sanford&nbsp;site are&nbsp;currently projected to be&nbsp;approximately
$727,000.&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Pensacola
Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are the prior owner/operator of the former Pensacola gasification plant, located
in Pensacola, Florida. Following notification on October 5, 1990 that FDEP had
determined that we were one of several responsible parties for any environmental
impacts associated with the former gasification plant site, we entered into cost
sharing agreements with three other responsible parties providing for the
funding of certain contamination assessment activities at the site.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Consulting
and remediation costs are projected to be $26,000 and legal fees are projected
to be $4,000, for total probable costs for the Pensacola site of $30,000.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Key
West Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">From
1927-1938, we owned and operated a gasification plant in Key West, Florida. The
plant discontinued operations in the late 1940s; the property on which the plant
was located is currently used for a propane gas distribution business. In March
1993, a Preliminary Contamination Assessment Report (PCAR) was prepared by a
consultant jointly retained by the current site owner and the Company and was
delivered to FDEP. The PCAR reported that very limited soil and groundwater
impacts were present at the site. By letter dated December 20, 1993, FDEP
notified us that the site did not warrant further "CERCLA consideration and a
Site Evaluation Accomplished disposition is recommended." FDEP then referred the
matter to its Marathon office for consideration of whether additional work would
be required by FDEP's district office under Florida law. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-RIGHT: 1px; PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Consulting
and remediation costs are projected to be $83,000 and legal fees are projected
to be $10,000, for total probable costs for the Key West site of $93,000.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
4.</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 66px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Submission
of Matters to a Vote of Security Holders</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 28px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify>None</P>

of these executive officers has an employment agreement dated March 31, 2006
with a term through March 30, 2009, which can be renewed by the Board prior to
the expiration of the agreement subject to earlier resignation or removal.
&nbsp;There are no family relationships among any of the executive officers and
directors of the Company. </P>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
intend to pay quarterly cash dividends for the foreseeable future. Our dividend
policy is reviewed on an ongoing basis by our Board of Directors and is
dependent upon future earnings, cash flow, financial condition, capital
requirements and other factors. &nbsp;Our Fifteenth Supplemental Indenture of
Mortgage and Deed of Trust restricts the amount that is available for cash
dividends. &nbsp;At December 31, 2007, approximately $9.6 million of retained
earnings were free of such restriction and available for the payment of
dividends.</P>

<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 8pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Note
to the Selected Financial Data:</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -13px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(1)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">On
December 3, 2002, FPU entered into an agreement to sell the assets of its water
utility system to the City of Fernandina Beach. &nbsp;The transaction closed on
March 27, 2003. &nbsp;Revenues, Gross profit and Utility plant-net do not
include discontinued operations. </P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 66px; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;&nbsp;(2)
&nbsp;&nbsp;Prior period amounts have been reclassified to conform to current
year presentation.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -13px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(3)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">On
July 25, 2005 a three-for-two stock split in the form of a stock dividend was
issued to the shareholders of record on July 15, 2005. &nbsp;&nbsp;All common
share information has been restated to reflect the stock split for all periods
presented.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Item
7.</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 72px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Management&#146;s
Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of Operation</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>RESULTS
OF OPERATIONS</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>General
</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
effects of seasonal weather conditions, timing of rate increases, fluctuations
in demand due to the cost of fuel passed on to customers, and the migration of
winter residents and tourists to Florida during the winter season, have a
significant impact on income.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 30px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Revenues
and Gross Profit Summary</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
FPSC allows us to bill and include in our revenue the costs of fuel,
conservation, and revenue-based taxes, incurred in our natural gas and electric
segments. Revenues collected for these expenses have no effect on results of
operations and fluctuations could distort the relationship of revenues between
periods. Gross profit is defined as gross operating revenues less fuel,
conservation and revenue-based taxes that are passed directly through to
customers. Because gross profit eliminates these cost recovery revenues, we
believe it provides a more meaningful basis for evaluating utility revenues. The
following summary compares gross profit, units sold, and average customers for
the past three years. &nbsp;Units sold are shown in one thousand Dekatherm
(MDth) for gas and Megawatt Hour (MWH) for electric.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Natural
Gas</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Natural
gas revenues decreased $6.3 million, or 9% in 2007 over 2006. As the cost of
natural gas continued to decline, the revenue to recover the fuel costs, which
are passed through to customers, decreased by $5.7 million. &nbsp;Our gross
profit, which excludes expenses directly passed through to customers, decreased
by $631,000 or 2%. &nbsp;Although customer growth was up in 2007 compared to
2006, we experienced a 3% decrease in units sold primarily due to milder
weather. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Natural
gas revenues increased $2.0 million, or 3% in 2006 over 2005 primarily due to
increased revenue collected for taxes passed directly through to customers. A
change in legislation regarding the calculation of Gross Receipts tax became
effective January 1, 2006, and along with an increase to overall revenues,
increased these taxes paid by our customers by approximately $500,000.
&nbsp;Franchise fee revenues also increased by approximately $500,000 due to
increased rates and area expansion.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 6px; PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Natural
gas gross profit increased $951,000, or 4% in 2006 over 2005. We had higher
revenue and gross profit in 2006 compared to 2005 primarily due to billed
revenue not exceeding the FPSC allowable earnings as much as in the prior year.
In 2006, we reduced billed revenues and gross profit by our estimate of
over-earnings of $72,000 for the year. Our estimate for 2005 was recorded at
$700,000 in 2005 and we reduced that estimate in 2006 by $50,000 to $650,000.
The combined effect of this was to increase our revenues and gross profit over
the prior year by approximately $678,000. Other factors contributing to the
increase in revenues and gross profit were 2% customer growth and storm
surcharge revenues, which became effective November 2005. The revenues and gross
profit increases were slightly offset by the loss of approximately $100,000 of
revenue from two customers who went off-line for several months to do
maintenance work.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 6px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Electric</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Electric
revenues increased $7.0 million or 14% in 2007 over 2006. &nbsp;Cost of fuel and
other costs that were passed through to customers contributed to $6.9 million of
the increase as a result of the new fuel contracts effective January 1, 2007 in
our Northeast division. Gross profit, which excludes the fuel and other costs
passed through in revenue, was flat compared to 2006. &nbsp;Although the number
of customers increased by 1%, there was a marginal decrease in units sold,
excluding units sold to industrial customers, as a result of possible
conservation measures taken by our customers due to the fuel cost increases.</P>
<P style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 7px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 29px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Electric
revenues increased $1.1 million in 2006 over 2005. &nbsp;Cost of fuel and other
costs that were passed through to customers contributed approximately $900,000
of the increase. Gross profit increased $170,000 or 1% in 2006 over 2005.
&nbsp;The increase in gross profit was primarily due to a slight increase in
customer growth and units sold.</P>
<P style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 7px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Propane
Gas</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Propane
revenues increased $1.1 million, or 7%. Higher fuel costs caused $625,000 of
this increase. Gross profit increased $431,000 or 6% in 2007 compared to 2006.
&nbsp;We experienced a 4% decrease in units sold to customers&nbsp;due to warmer
weather; however, this was offset by increased rates and service fees.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Propane
revenues increased $1.4 million, or 10% and gross profit increased $208,000 or
3% in 2006 compared to 2005. &nbsp;Revenues increased primarily due to rising
fuel costs. Although customers increased by 5% in 2006, the usage per customer
declined by 8% contributing to a decrease of 3% in units sold. &nbsp;Warmer
weather was the primary reason for this decrease in usage per customer in 2006
compared to 2005. The increase in gross profit was minimal when compared to 2005
primarily due to pre-buy gains of $383,000 realized in 2005 but not in 2006.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Operating
Expenses </B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 30px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Operating
expenses include operation, maintenance, depreciation, amortization and taxes
other than income taxes, and exclude fuel costs, conservation and taxes based on
revenues that are directly passed through to customers and recovered in
revenues. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
