style="FONT-SIZE: 9pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 11pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=right>$16,045&nbsp;</P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(1)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Deferred
development costs, unamortized piping and conversion costs, and unamortized loss
on reacquired debt are included in deferred charges in the consolidated balance
sheets. </P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(2)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has included the amount due from customers as a regulatory asset for
environmental costs. The FPSC authorized recovery of these environmental costs
from customers over 20 years.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(3)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Commission ordered disposition of our 2005 over-earnings to eliminate the
related regulatory asset-storm reserve and the storm surcharge collected from
customers in our natural gas operations. The remaining over-earnings was used to
fund a storm reserve for future storm costs in our natural gas division.
&nbsp;Our natural gas storm reserve is approximately $613,000 as of December 31,
2007 as a result of this order.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(4)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
actuarial valuation of the retirement plan obligations has been completed and
the recognition provisions of Statement 158 at December 31, 2006 resulted in a
regulatory asset for the portion of the loss of $587,000 and at December 31,
2007 resulted in a regulatory liability for $564,000. </P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(5)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has deferred certain regulatory fuel-related costs and as of January
2006 has been amortizing these over five years<FONT color=#00007f>
</FONT>according to a FPSC order in the November 2005 fuel hearings.</P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(6)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has certain costs that are passed directly through to customers for
recovery including fuel and conservation costs. There are amounts related to
these expenses that are either over or under-recovered in a calendar year. These
over-recoveries will be returned to customers and under-recoveries will be
collected from customers in the following year, but both are deferred in the
current period. </P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(7)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has costs associated with preparing and filing a rate proceeding before
the FPSC. These costs are amortized over a four or five year period. This
represents the unamortized portion of these costs. The Company has incurred
additional rate case costs associated with the electric filing expected to be
finalized in 2008. The additional costs are expected to be amortized over a four
year period beginning mid 2008. </P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(8)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has deferred tax liabilities associated with property. The Company uses
a FPSC-approved method to amortize these liabilities. </P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(9)</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 114px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has a liability for the estimated future costs to remove or retire
existing fixed assets.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR>&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
base revenue rates for regulated segments are determined by the FPSC and remain
constant until a request for an increase is filed and approved by the FPSC or
the FPSC orders the Company to reduce their rates. &nbsp;For the Company to
recover increased costs from the effects of inflation and construction
expenditures for regulated segments, a request for an increase in base revenues
would be required. Separate filings would be required for the electric and
natural gas segments. &nbsp;The Company is currently seeking rate relief in
their electric segment, and approval, if any, is expected in the second quarter
of 2008.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">At
December 31, 2007,&nbsp;all&nbsp;of our regulatory assets and all of our
regulatory liabilities are reflected or are expected to be reflected in rates
charged to customers.&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Criteria
that give rise to the discontinuance of SFAS 71 include increasing competition
that restricts our ability to establish prices to recover specific costs, and a
significant change in the manner in which rates are set by regulators from
cost-based regulation to another form of regulation.&nbsp; We periodically
review these criteria to ensure that the&nbsp;continuing application of SFAS 71
is appropriate.&nbsp; Based on a current evaluation of the various factors and
conditions that are expected to impact future cost recovery, we believe that our
regulatory assets are probable of recovery.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>F. Derivatives</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">None
of the Company&#146;s gas or electric contracts are accounted for using the fair
value method of accounting. All material contracts that meet the definition of
derivative instruments are considered "normal purchases and sales" under SFAS
No. 133, "Accounting for Derivative Instruments and Hedging Activities&#148;.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>G. Revenue Recognition</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company&#146;s revenues consist of base revenues, fuel adjustment charges,
conservation charges and the pass-through of certain governmental imposed taxes
based on revenues.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
FPSC approves base revenue rates that are intended to permit a specified rate of
return on investment and limits the maximum amount of earnings of regulated
operations. &nbsp;&nbsp;Fuel adjustment charges, conservation charges and the
pass-through of certain governmental imposed taxes based on revenues are
approved by the FPSC to allow recovery of fuel, conservation and revenue based
taxes from the Company&#146;s customers. &nbsp;Any over or under-recovery of these
expense items are deferred and subsequently refunded or collected in the
following period.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Annually,
any earnings in excess of this maximum amount permitted in the base rates are
accrued for as an over-earning liability and revenues are reduced an equivalent
amount. The calculations supporting these liabilities are complex and involve a
variety of projections and estimates before the ultimate settlement of such
obligations. The FPSC determines the disposition of any over-earnings with
alternatives that include refunding to customers, funding storm damage or
environmental reserves, or reducing any depreciation reserve deficiency.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company bills utility customers on a monthly cycle basis; however, the billing
cycle periods for most customers do not coincide with the accounting periods
used for financial reporting. &nbsp;The Company accrues estimated revenue for
gas and electric customers on usage not yet billed for the accounting period.
Determination of unbilled revenue relies on the use of estimates, fuel purchases
and historical data.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Interim
rate relief for partial recovery of the increased expenditures was approved by
the Commission on October 23, 2007. Interim rates which should produce
additional annual revenues of approximately $800,000 went into effect for meter
readings on and after November 22, 2007. The permanent rates may differ from the
interim rates, and the interim rates are collected subject to refund with
interest.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>H. </B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: -6px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>Taxes Collected from Customers and Remitted to Governmental
Authorities</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify>The Company remits to governmental authorities various taxes
collected from customers throughout the year including gross receipts and
franchise taxes. These taxes are pass through revenues and expenses and do not
impact the Company&#146;s results of operations. The amount of gross receipts and
franchise taxes for the year ending December 31, 2007 and 2006 was $7.1 million
and $6.9 million, respectively.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>I.
Allowance for Doubtful Accounts</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company records an allowance for doubtful accounts based on historical
information and trended current economic conditions. &nbsp;The following is a
summary of the activity in Allowance for Doubtful Accounts for the years ending
December 31:</P>

<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: -25px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
J. Utility Plant and Depreciation</B> </P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Utility
plant is stated at original cost. &nbsp;The propane gas utility plant that was
acquired through acquisitions was stated at fair market value when acquired.
&nbsp;Additions to utility plant include contracted services, direct labor,
transportation and materials for additions. &nbsp;Units of property are removed
from utility plant when retired. &nbsp;Maintenance and repairs of property and
replacement and renewal of items determined not to be units of property are
charged to operating expenses. &nbsp;Substantially all of the utility plant and
the shares of Flo-Gas Corporation collateralize the Company's first mortgage
bonds.</P>

<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Depreciation
for the Company&#146;s regulated segments is computed using the composite
straight-line method at rates prescribed by the FPSC for financial accounting
purposes. &nbsp;Propane gas depreciation is computed using a composite
straight-line method at an average rate based on estimated average life of
approximately 20-30 years. &nbsp;Such rates are based on estimated service lives
of the various classes of property. &nbsp;Depreciation provisions on average
depreciable property approximate 3.8% in 2007, 3.9% in 2006 and 3.9% in 2005.
Depreciation expense was $6.7 million, $6.2 million and $5.7 million for 2007,
2006 and 2005, respectively.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 60px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>K.
Impact of Recent Accounting Standards</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 12.75pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Financial
Accounting Standard No. 157</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
September 2006, the FASB issued Statement No. 157, &#147;Fair Value Measurements&#148;.
&nbsp;This Statement clarifies fair value as the market value received to sell
an asset or paid to transfer a liability, that is, the exit value, and applies
to any assets or liabilities that require recurring determination of fair value.
&nbsp;The measurement includes any applicable risk factors and does not include
any adjustment for volume. &nbsp;On February&nbsp;12, 2008, the FASB issued
proposed FASB Staff Position No.&nbsp;FAS No.&nbsp;157-2, &#147;Effective Date of
FASB Statement No.&nbsp;157&#148; which defers the effective date for all
nonfinancial assets and nonfinancial liabilities, except those that are
recognized or disclosed at fair value in the financial statements on a recurring
basis (that is, at least annually) to fiscal years beginning after
November&nbsp;15, 2008. The Company expects to adopt SFAS No. 157 effective
January 1, 2009. The Company is still evaluating the impact adoption of this
Statement will have on our financial condition or results of operation.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Financial
Accounting Standard No. 159</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
February 2007, the FASB issued Statement of Financial Accounting Standard (SFAS)
No. 159, &#147;The Fair Value Option for Financial Assets and Financial Liabilities&#148;.
&nbsp;This Statement permits measurement at fair value of certain firm
commitments, nonfinancial insurance contracts and warranties, host financial
instruments and recognized financial assets and liabilities, excluding
consolidating investments in subsidiaries, consolidating variable interest
entities, various forms of deferred compensation agreements, leases, depository
institution deposit liabilities and financial instruments included in
shareholders&#146; equity. &nbsp;This Statement is effective for financial statements
issued for fiscal years beginning after November 15, 2007. The Company does not
expect to adopt SFAS No. 159.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; MARGIN: 0px; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Financial
Accounting Standard No. 160</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-RIGHT: 24px; PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
December 2007, the FASB issued Statement No. 160, &#147;Noncontrolling Interests in
Consolidated Financial Statements &#150; an amendment of ARB No. 51&#148;.&nbsp; This
standard requires noncontrolling ownership interests be disclosed separately in
equity, separate disclosure of income contributable to each party, changes in
controlling interests be reported consistently, and deconsolidation be measured
at fair value. As the company does not&nbsp;currently&nbsp;have any
noncontrolling interests this standard will not have an impact on our financial
condition or results of operations until the Company acquires a noncontrolling
interest.</P>
<P style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; MARGIN: 0px; FONT-FAMILY: Courier New">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; MARGIN: 0px; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Financial
Accounting Standard No. 141R</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
December 2007, the FASB issued a revision to Statement No. 141, &#147;Business
Combinations&#148;. This statement is effective prospectively for business
combinations occurring on or after January 1, 2009 for our company.&nbsp; This
revision broadens the scope of a business combination to include transactions in
which no consideration has been exchanged, sets the acquisition date as the date
control is obtained, replaces the cost allocation method with fair value method
to assign values to assets and liabilities assumed, requires restructuring costs
to be recorded separate of the business combination, and does not permit
deferral of contractual contingencies at acquisition date.&nbsp; As this
revision is adopted prospectively and all qualifying future business
combinations would be evaluated under the new provisions, the effects on our
results of operations will depend on the nature and&nbsp;size of&nbsp;any future
acquisitions.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 60px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>L.
Earnings Per Share</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 96px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company includes earnings per common share (basic and diluted) on the
consolidated statements of income. The Company does not have dilutive or
anti-dilutive shares.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>2.</B>
&nbsp;<B>Goodwill and Intangible Assets</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 36px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
accordance with SFAS No. 142, "Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets", the
Company does not amortize goodwill or intangibles with indefinite lives.
&nbsp;The Company periodically tests the applicable reporting segments, natural
gas and propane gas, for impairment. In the event a segment becomes impaired,
the Company would write-down the associated goodwill and intangible assets with
indefinite lives to fair value. The impairment tests performed in 2006 and 2007
showed no impairment for either reporting segment.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 36px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Goodwill
associated with the Company&#146;s acquisitions is identified as a separate line item
on the consolidated balance sheet and consists of $1.9 million in the propane
gas segment and $500,000 in the natural gas segment.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 36px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Intangible
assets associated with the Company&#146;s acquisitions and software have been
identified as a separate line item on the balance sheet. &nbsp;Summaries of
those intangible assets at December 31 are as follows:</P>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>3.<I>
&nbsp;</I>Over-earnings-Natural Gas</B> </P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
FPSC approves rates that are intended to permit a specified rate of return on
investment and limits the maximum amount of earnings of regulated operations.
The Company has agreed with the FPSC staff to limit the earned return on equity
for regulated natural gas and electric operations.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
2007, there were no estimated natural gas over-earnings.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company recorded estimated 2006 over-earnings for regulated natural gas
operations of $25,000. Interest accrued on this estimated over-earnings as of
December 31, 2007 is $1,300. This liability is included in the over-earnings
liability on the Company&#146;s consolidated balance sheet of December 31, 2007. The
calculations supporting these liabilities are complex and involve a variety of
projections and estimates before the ultimate settlement of such obligations.
Estimates may be revised as expectations change and factors become known and
determinable.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
2006 over-earnings liability is based on the Company&#146;s best estimate, but the
amount could change upon the FPSC finalization expected in 2008. The FPSC
determines the disposition of over-earnings with alternatives that include
refunding to customers, increasing storm damage or environmental reserves or
reducing any depreciation reserve deficiency.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">On
August 14, 2007 the Commission finalized the disposition of 2005 over-earnings
for the natural gas segment. Total over-earnings was determined to be $666,000,
plus interest of $76,000. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Commission ordered disposition of 2005 over-earnings to eliminate the related
regulatory asset-storm reserve in natural gas operations and the storm surcharge
collected from customers. The remaining over-earnings was used to fund a storm
reserve for any future storm costs. &nbsp;</P>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>4.
&nbsp;Storm Reserves</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">As
of December 31, 2007, the Company had a storm reserve of approximately $1.8
million for the electric segment and approximately $613,000 for the natural gas
segment. The Company does not have a storm reserve for the propane gas segment.
</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">As
noted above, in the August 2007 the Commission ordered disposition of 2005
over-earnings to eliminate the related regulatory asset &#150; storm reserve in
natural gas operations and the storm surcharge collected from customers. The
remaining over-earnings was used to fund a storm reserve for any future storm
costs.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>5.
&nbsp;Income Taxes</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Financial
Accounting Standard Board Interpretation No. 48</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
June 2006, the FASB issued Interpretation No. 48, Accounting for Uncertainty in
Income Taxes (FIN 48).&nbsp; The interpretation clarifies the accounting for
uncertainty in income taxes recognized in an entity&#146;s financial statements in
accordance with SFAS No. 109, Accounting for Income Taxes.&nbsp; The
interpretation prescribes a recognition threshold and measurement attribute for
the financial statement recognition and measurement of a tax position taken or
expected to be taken in a tax return. FIN 48 also provides guidance on
de-recognition, measurement, classification, interest and penalties, accounting
in interim periods, disclosure and transition.&nbsp; This interpretation is
effective for calendar years beginning January 1, 2007.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">At
January 1, 2007, we performed an analysis of tax positions taken and expected to
be taken on the tax returns and assessed the technical merits of each tax
position by relying on legislation, statutes, common legislative intent,
regulations, rulings and case law and determined&nbsp;that the Company has no
material uncertain tax positions.&nbsp;&nbsp; </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
February of 2007, the IRS selected our 2003 and 2004 tax years for examination.
As of December 31, 2007, the IRS examination was not complete. &nbsp;We
performed an assessment of our uncertain tax positions as of December 31, 2007,
and recognized a FIN 48 liability for various tax positions relating solely to
the timing of various tax deductions. &nbsp;A disallowance of the shorter
deductibility period for these tax positions would not affect the annual
effective income tax rate. &nbsp;These tax positions relate to the 2004 through
2007 tax years. &nbsp;The effects of these tax positions are disclosed in the
reconciliation below.</P>

February 2008, the IRS submitted its Notice of Proposed Adjustment to us.
&nbsp;We have reviewed and expect to agree to the IRS proposed audit adjustments
in March 2008. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">It
is reasonably possible that a liability associated with uncertain tax positions
may arise within the next twelve months. &nbsp;These changes may be the result
of the ongoing IRS audit, the expiration of statutes of limitations or from
other developments. At this time an estimate of reasonably possible outcomes
cannot be made. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are subject to taxation in the United States and the State of Florida. &nbsp;Our
tax years from 2004 through 2007 are subject to examination by the tax
authorities.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company&#146;s policy regarding interest and penalties related to income tax matters
is to recognize such items separately and not as components of income tax
expense. &nbsp;For the year ended December 31, 2007 we have recognized $44,000
in interest expense and accrued interest and no penalty expense related to
income tax matters.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Deferred
income taxes are provided on all significant temporary differences between the
financial statements and tax basis of assets and liabilities at currently
enacted tax rates. &nbsp;Investment tax credits have been deferred and are
amortized based upon the average useful life of the related property in
accordance with the rate treatment.</P>

value and $22 redemption price, authorized for issuance none of which has been
issued.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>C.
Dividend Restriction</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company&#146;s Fifteenth Supplemental Indenture of Mortgage and Deed of Trust
restricts the amount that is available for cash dividends. &nbsp;At December 31,
2007, approximately $9.6 million of retained earnings were free of such
restriction and therefore available for the payment of dividends. &nbsp;The line
of credit agreement contains covenants that, if violated, could restrict or
prevent the payment of dividends. As of December 31, 2007 the Company was not in
violation of these covenants.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>D.
Employee Stock Purchase Plan</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company&#146;s Employee Stock Purchase Plan offers common stock at a discount to
qualified employees.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>E.
Dividend Reinvestment Plan</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company&#146;s Dividend Reinvestment Plan is offered to all Company shareholders and
allows the shareholder to reinvest dividends received and purchase additional
shares without a fee. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>7.
&nbsp;Long-term Debt</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company issued its Fourteenth Series of FPU&#146;s First Mortgage Bond on September
27, 2001 in the aggregate principal amount of $15 million as security for the
6.85% Secured Insured Quarterly Notes, due October 1, 2031 (IQ Notes).
&nbsp;Interest on the pledged bond accrues at the annual rate of 6.85% payable
quarterly in arrears on January 1, April 1, July 1 and October 1 of each year
beginning January 1, 2002.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company issued $14 million of Palm Beach County municipal bonds (Industrial
Development Revenue Bonds) on November 14, 2001 to finance development in the
area. &nbsp;The interest rate on the thirty-year callable bonds is 4.90%.
&nbsp;The bond proceeds were restricted and held in trust until construction
expenditures were actually incurred by the Company. &nbsp;In 2002 the remaining
$8 million was drawn from the restricted funds held by the trustee.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
1992, the Company issued its First Mortgage Bond 9.08% Series in the amount of
$8 million. The thirty-year bond is due in June 2022.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company issued two of its Twelfth Series First Mortgage bond series on May 1,
1988; the 9.57% Series due 2018 in the amount of $10 million and 10.03% Series
due 2018 in the amount of $5.5 million. &nbsp;These two issuances require
sinking fund payments of $909,000 and $500,000 respectively, beginning in
2008.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Long-term
debt on the balance sheet has been reduced for unamortized debt discount. The
unamortized debt discount at December 31 included in long-term debt on the
balance sheet is $1.7 million in 2007 and $1.8 million in 2006.</P>

<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>8.
&nbsp;Line of Credit</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
2004, FPU entered into an amended and restated loan agreement that allows the
Company to increase the line of credit upon 30 days notice by the Company to a
maximum of $20 million. &nbsp;In 2006 the agreement was renewed with an
expiration date of July 1, 2008. We have not exercised our option to increase
the line of credit limit which is currently at $12 million with an outstanding
balance of $11.1 million. &nbsp;&nbsp;The Company reserves $1 million of the
line of credit to cover expenses for any major storm repairs in its electric
segment. &nbsp;An additional $250,000 of the line of credit is reserved for a
&#145;letter of credit&#146; insuring our propane facilities. In March 2008, we amended
our line of credit to allow us, upon 30 days notice, to increase our maximum
credit line to $26 million. The new agreement expires July 1, 2010. The
amendment also reduces the interest rate paid on borrowings by .10% or 10 basis
points. The new interest rate terms, if effective for 2007, would have reduced
our overall average interest rate for 2007 to approximately 5.7% from 5.8% as of
December 31, 2007.<BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
average interest rates for the line of credit were as follows as of December
31:</P>

style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The

for environmental costs, notes payable, taxes accrued and other accrued
liabilities approximate fair value. &nbsp;The fair value of long-term debt
excluding the unamortized debt discount is estimated by discounting the future
cash flows of each issuance at rates currently offered to the Company for
similar debt instruments of comparable maturities. The indentures governing our
two first mortgage bond series outstanding contain "make-whole" provisions
(pre-payment penalties that charge for lost interest). The values at December 31
are shown below.</P>

<P

align=justify><B>10. &nbsp;Contingencies</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px 0px 0px -15px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Environmental
</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px 0px 0px -15px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company is subject to federal and state legislation with respect to soil,
groundwater and employee health and safety matters and to environmental
regulations issued by the Florida Department of Environmental Protection (FDEP),
the United States Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) and other federal and
state agencies. Except as discussed below, the Company does not expect to
incur<FONT color=#007f7f> </FONT>material future expenditures for compliance
with existing environmental laws and regulations.</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px 0px 0px -15px; TEXT-INDENT: 192px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">(Dollars
in thousands)</P>

<TR style="FONT-SIZE: 0px">
<TD width=203></TD>
<TD width=142></TD>
<TD width=124></TD></TR>
<TR>
<TD vAlign=bottom width=203>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 9pt; MARGIN: 0px 0px 0px -15px; LINE-HEIGHT: 11pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Site</B></P></TD>
<TD vAlign=bottom width=142>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 9pt; MARGIN: 0px 0px 0px -15px; LINE-HEIGHT: 11pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=right><B>Range From</B></P></TD>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE style="FONT-SIZE: 10pt" cellSpacing=0>
<TR style="FONT-SIZE: 0px">
<TD width=203></TD>
<TD width=142></TD>
<TD width=124></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company currently has $13.6 million recorded as our best estimate of the
environmental liability.<FONT color=#ff0000> </FONT>The FPSC approved up to $14
million for total recovery from insurance and rates based on the original 2005
projections as a basis for rate recovery.<FONT color=#ff0000> </FONT>The Company
has recovered a total of $6 million<FONT color=#ff0000> </FONT>from insurance
and rate recovery, net of costs incurred to date. &nbsp;The remaining balance of
$7.6 million is recorded as a regulatory asset.<FONT color=#ff0000>
&nbsp;</FONT>On October 18, 2004 the FPSC approved recovery of $9.1 million for
environmental liabilities. &nbsp;The amortization of this recovery and reduction
to the regulatory asset began on January 1, 2005. The majority of environmental
cash expenditures is expected to be incurred before 2010, but may continue for
another 10 years.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>West
Palm Beach Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company is currently evaluating remedial options to respond to environmental
impacts to soil and groundwater at and in the immediate vicinity of a parcel of
property owned by it in West Palm Beach, Florida upon which the Company
previously operated a gasification plant. The Company entered into a Consent
Order with the FDEP effective April 8, 1991, that requires the Company to
delineate the extent of soil and groundwater impacts associated with the prior
operation of the gasification plant and to remediate such soil and groundwater
impacts, if necessary. The Company completed field investigations for the
contamination assessment task in October 2006.&nbsp; Thereafter, The Company
retained an engineering consultant, The RETEC Group, Inc. (RETEC), to perform a
feasibility study to evaluate appropriate remedies for the site to respond to
the reported soil and groundwater impacts.&nbsp; On&nbsp;November 30,
2006,&nbsp;RETEC transmitted a feasibility study to the Company and FDEP.&nbsp;
The feasibility study evaluated a wide range of remedial alternatives.&nbsp;The
total costs for the remedies evaluated in the feasibility study ranged from a
low of $2.8 million to a high of $54.6 million.&nbsp; Based on the likely
acceptability of proven remedial technologies described in the feasibility study
and implemented at similar sites, consulting/remediation costs are projected to
range from $4.6 million to $17.9 million. This range of costs covers such
remedies as in situ solidification for the deeper impacts,&nbsp;excavation of
surficial soils,&nbsp;installation of a barrier wall with a permeable
biotreatment zone, or some combination of these remedies.&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">By
letter dated May 7, 2007, FDEP provided its comments to the&nbsp;feasibility
study, the substance of which was discussed at a meeting between the Company and
FDEP on September 14, 2007.&nbsp;&nbsp;A response to&nbsp;the comments was
submitted by the Company to FDEP on October 31, 2007.&nbsp; We are currently
awaiting FDEP's comments to the response.&nbsp; </P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Based
on the&nbsp;information provided in the feasibility study,&nbsp;remaining legal
fees are currently projected to be&nbsp;approximately $295,000. Consulting and
remediation costs are projected to range from $4.6 million to $17.9
million.&nbsp;Thus, the Company's total probable legal and cleanup
costs&nbsp;for the West Palm Beach site are&nbsp;currently projected&nbsp;to
range&nbsp;from $4.9 million to $18.2 million. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Sanford
Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company owns a parcel of property located in Sanford, Florida, upon which a
gasification plant was operated prior to the Company&#146;s acquisition of the
property. Following discovery of soil and groundwater impacts on the property,
the Company has participated with four former owners and operators of the
gasification plant in the funding of numerous investigations of the extent of
the impacts and the identification of an appropriate remedy. On or about March
25, 1998, the Company executed an Administrative Order on Consent (AOC) with the
four former owners and operators (collectively, the Group) and the EPA. This AOC
obligated the Group to implement a Remedial Investigation/Feasibility Study
(RI/FS) and to pay EPA's past and future oversight costs. The Group also entered
into a Participation Agreement and an Escrow Agreement on or about April 13,
1998 (WFS Participation Agreement). Work under the RI/FS AOC and RI/FS
Participation Agreement is now complete and the Company has no further
obligations under either agreement.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In&nbsp;late
September&nbsp;2006, the EPA&nbsp;sent a Special Notice Letter to&nbsp;the
Company, notifying it, and the other responsible parties at the site (Florida
Power Corporation, Florida Power &amp; Light Company, Atlanta Gas Light Company,
and the City of Sanford, Florida, collectively with FPUC, "the
Sanford&nbsp;Group"), of EPA's selection of a final remedy for OU1 (soils), OU2
(groundwater), and OU3 (sediments) for the site.&nbsp; The&nbsp;total estimated
remediation costs for the Sanford gasification plant site are now projected to
be $12.9 million.&nbsp;The Sanford Group was further advised that the EPA was
willing to negotiate a consent decree with the Sanford Group to provide for the
implementation of the&nbsp;final remedy approved by the EPA for the
site.&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
January 2007, the Company&nbsp;and other members of the Sanford Group signed a
Third Participation Agreement, which provides for funding the&nbsp;final remedy
approved by the EPA for the site. &nbsp;The Company's share of remediation
costs&nbsp;under the Third Participation Agreement is set at a maximum of
$650,000, providing the total cost of the&nbsp;final remedy&nbsp;does not exceed
$13 million.&nbsp; At present, it is not anticipated that the total cost will
exceed $13 million.&nbsp; If it does, the Sanford Group&nbsp;members have agreed
to negotiate in good faith at such time that it appears that the total cost will
exceed $13 million for the allocation of the additional cost.&nbsp;&nbsp;The
Company&nbsp;has advised the other members of the Sanford Group that the Company
is unwilling at this time to agree to pay any sum in excess of the $650,000
committed by the Company in the Third Participation Agreement. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">On
June 26, 2007, the Sanford Group transmitted to the EPA a consent decree signed
by all Group Members, providing for the implementation by the Sanford Group of
the remedy selected by the EPA&nbsp;for the&nbsp;site.&nbsp;&nbsp;The consent
decree is currently being circulated within the EPA and the United States
Department of Justice for execution by those parties.&nbsp; Thereafter, the
consent decree will be lodged with the federal court in Orlando, Florida.&nbsp;
Following a public comment period, it is anticipated that the federal court will
enter the consent decree.&nbsp; The Sanford Group will then be obligated to
implement the remedy approved by the EPA for the&nbsp;site.&nbsp; </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Remaining
legal fees and costs are&nbsp;currently projected to be&nbsp;approximately
$77,000.&nbsp;The Company's obligation under the Third Participation Agreement
is $650,000. Thus,<FONT color=#007f7f> </FONT>the Company's total probable legal
and cleanup costs&nbsp;for the Sanford&nbsp;site are&nbsp;currently projected to
be&nbsp;approximately $727,000.&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Pensacola
Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
are the prior owner/operator of the former Pensacola gasification plant, located
in Pensacola, Florida. Following notification on October 5, 1990 that FDEP had
determined that we were one of several responsible parties for any environmental
impacts associated with the former gasification plant site, we entered into cost
sharing agreements with three other responsible parties providing for the
funding of certain contamination assessment activities at the site.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Consulting
and remediation costs are projected to be $26,000 and legal fees are projected
to be $4,000, for total probable costs for the Pensacola site of $30,000.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Key
West Site</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">From
1927-1938, we owned and operated a gasification plant in Key West, Florida. The
plant discontinued operations in the late 1940s; the property on which the plant
was located is currently used for a propane gas distribution business. In March
1993, a Preliminary Contamination Assessment Report (PCAR) was prepared by a
consultant jointly retained by the current site owner and the Company and was
delivered to FDEP. The PCAR reported that very limited soil and groundwater
impacts were present at the site. By letter dated December 20, 1993, FDEP
notified us that the site did not warrant further "CERCLA consideration and a
Site Evaluation Accomplished disposition is recommended." FDEP then referred the
matter to its Marathon office for consideration of whether additional work would
be required by FDEP's district office under Florida law. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-RIGHT: 1px; PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Consulting
and remediation costs are projected to be $83,000 and legal fees are projected
to be $10,000, for total probable costs for the Key West site of $93,000.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>11.
&nbsp;Commitments</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>A.
General</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">To
ensure a reliable supply of electric and natural gas at competitive prices, the
Company has entered into long-term purchase and transportation contracts with
various suppliers and producers, which expire at various dates through
2023.&nbsp;&nbsp;At December 31, 2007, the Company has firm purchase and
transportation commitments adequate to supply its expected future sales
requirements. The Company is committed to pay demand or similar fixed charges of
approximately $41.9 million during 2008 related to gas purchase agreements.
&nbsp;Substantially all costs incurred under the electric and gas purchase
agreements are currently recoverable from customers through fuel adjustment
clause mechanisms.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>12.
&nbsp;Employee Benefit Plans</B> </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
actuarial valuation of the retirement plan obligations has been completed and
the recognition provisions of Statement 158 resulting in an additional liability
for retirement plans, pension plan and retirees&#146; medical plan have been
recorded. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>A.
Pension Plan</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
September 2006, the FASB issued Statement No. 158, &#147;Employers&#146; Accounting for
Defined Benefit Pension and Other Postretirement Plans&#148;. Statement 158 requires
the Company to show the funded status of its pension and retiree health care
plans as a prepaid asset or accrued liability, and to show the net deferred and
unrecognized gains and losses related to the retirement plans, net of tax, as
part of accumulated other comprehensive income in shareholders&#146; equity.
&nbsp;Previously, the net deferred and unrecognized gains and losses were netted
in the prepaid asset or accrued liability recorded for the retirement plans.
</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: normal; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Our
Company adopted the recognition provisions of Statement 158, as required, at
December 31, 2006 and used December 31 as the measurement date to measure the
assets and obligations of its retirement plans.<FONT face="Courier New">
</FONT>This resulted in an additional liability for retirement plans.<FONT
face="Courier New"> </FONT>The&nbsp;tax on the non-regulated portion of the
liability has been recorded as&nbsp;a deferred income tax&nbsp;asset/liability.
As an offset,&nbsp;the regulatory portion of this liability has been deferred as
a regulatory asset/liability<FONT color=#007f7f>&nbsp;</FONT>to be recovered in
future rate proceedings and the remaining income/loss has been included in other
accumulated comprehensive income/loss.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
fair value of our retirement plan assets and obligations are subject to change
based on market fluctuations.<FONT color=#ff0000> </FONT></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company sponsors a qualified defined benefit pension plan for non-union
employees that were hired before January 1, 2005 and for unionized employees
that work under one of the six Company union contracts and were hired before
their respective contract dates in 2005.</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; COLOR: #ff0000; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
following tables provide a reconciliation of the changes in the plan's benefit
obligations and fair value of assets over the 3-year period ending December 31,
2007<FONT color=#007f7f> </FONT>and a statement of the funded status as of
December 31, of all three years:</P>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Expected
Return on Plan Assets</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
expected rate of return on plan assets is 8.5%. &nbsp;The Company expects 8.5%
to fall within the 50 to 60 percentile range of returns on investment portfolios
with asset diversification similar to that of the Pension Plan's target asset
allocation.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Investment
Policy and Strategy</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has established and maintains an investment policy designed to achieve a
long-term rate of return, including investment income and appreciation,
sufficient to meet the actuarial requirements of the Pension Plan. The Company
seeks to accomplish its return objectives by investing in a diversified
portfolio of equity, fixed income and cash securities seeking a balance of
growth and stability as well as an adequate level of liquidity for pension
distributions as they fall due. &nbsp;Plan assets are constrained such that no
more than 10% of the portfolio will be invested in any one issue.</P>

<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Discount
Rate Assumption</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
discount rate assumption used to determine the postretirement benefit
obligations is based on current yield rates in the double A bond market.
&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
current year&#146;s discount rate was selected using a method that matches projected
payouts from the plan with a zero-coupon double A bond yield curve. &nbsp;This
yield curve was constructed from the underlying bond price and yield data
collected as of the plan&#146;s measurement date and is represented by a series of
annualized, individual discount rates with durations ranging from six months to
thirty years. Each discount rate in the curve was derived from an equal
weighting of the double A or higher bond universe, apportioned into distinct
maturity groups. &nbsp;These individual discount rates are then converted into a
single equivalent discount rate, which is then used for FAS discount purposes.
To assure that the resulting rates can be achieved by a postretirement benefit
plan, only bonds that satisfy certain criteria and are expected to remain
available through the period of maturity of the plan benefits are used to
develop the discount rate. &nbsp;Prior years&#146; discount rate assumptions were set
based on investment yields available on double A, long-term corporate bonds.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Actuarial
Equivalent</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
determining "Actuarial Equivalence," a prescription drug pricing tool was used.
This tool allowed us to determine the estimated Per Member Per Month (PMPM)
prescription drug cost for both the Company plan and the Medicare plan.
&nbsp;The two PMPM's were adjusted for monthly retiree contributions. &nbsp;We
assumed that 60% of the monthly combined medical and prescription drug retiree
contribution for the Company plan applies towards prescription drugs.<FONT
face="Courier New"> </FONT>Because the subsidy is the same regardless of the
cost sharing structure (unless the plan is not "Actuarial Equivalent"), in
general a plan that has higher cost sharing would reduce their annual cost as a
percentage greater than a plan would that has lower cost sharing.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B><I>Voluntary
Prescription Drug Coverage</I></B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Legislation
enacted in December 2003 provides for the addition of voluntary prescription
drug coverage under Medicare starting in 2006. &nbsp;The legislation also
provides for a 28% tax-free subsidy for each qualified covered retiree&#146;s drug
cost between certain thresholds if the employer&#146;s coverage is at least
actuarially equivalent to the standard Medicare drug benefit. &nbsp;Based on the
final regulations issued by the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services on
January 21, 2005, we determined our prescription drug coverage of the
Postretirement Medical Benefits plan to be actuarially equivalent to Medicare
Part D.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>C.
Health Plan</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
December 2003, the Company became fully insured for its employee and retiree&#146;s
medical insurance. Net health care benefits paid by the Company were
approximately $1.8 million in 2007, $1.7 million in 2006 and $1.6 million in
2005 excluding administrative and stop-loss insurance.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>D.
401K Plan</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company has discontinued eligibility to the defined benefit pension plan for all
new hires, and replaced it with a new 401K match.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: normal; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">For
new hires not eligible for the defined benefit pension plan, we established an
employer match to the employee&#146;s contribution to their 401K plans. It provides
for a company match of 50% for each dollar contributed by the employee, up to 6%
of their salary, for a Company contribution of up to 3%. Beginning in 2007, for
non-union employees the plan was enhanced to provide a company match of 100% for
the first 2% of an employee&#146;s contribution, and a match of 50% for the next 4%
of an employee&#146;s contribution, for a total company match of up to 4%. This
enhanced match was successfully negotiated with our six union contracts in 2007.
Employees are automatically enrolled at 3% contribution, with the option of
opting out, and are eligible for the company match after six months of
continuous service, with vesting of 100% after three years of continuous
service.&nbsp;&nbsp;<BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 48px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">E.
<B>Employee Stock Purchase Plan</B></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 72px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company offers an employee stock purchase plan to substantially all of its
employees. &nbsp;The plan offers a 15% discount on the Company&#146;s stock at market
price fixed six months prior to the date of purchase. &nbsp;The recorded stock
compensation expense relating to the Company&#146;s employee stock purchase plan is
not material.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>13.
&nbsp;&nbsp;Segment Information</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
Company is organized into two regulated business segments: natural gas and
electric, and one non-regulated business segment, propane gas. &nbsp;There are
no material inter-segment sales or transfers.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px">&nbsp;</P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Identifiable
assets are those assets used in the Company&#146;s operations in each business
segment. &nbsp;Common assets are principally cash and overnight investments,
deferred tax assets and common plant.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 48px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Business
segment information for 2007, 2006 and 2005 is summarized as follows:</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<TABLE style="FONT-SIZE: 10pt" cellSpacing=0>
<TR style="FONT-SIZE: 0px">

<TD width=90></TD>
<TD width=18></TD>

<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 36px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>Quarterly Financial Data (Unaudited)</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">The
quarterly financial data presented below reflects the influence of seasonal
weather conditions, the timing of rate increases and the migration of winter
residents and tourists to Central and South Florida during the winter
season.</P>

<P style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE: always; MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="PADDING-LEFT: 24px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: -24px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>REPORT OF INDEPENDENT REGISTERED PUBLIC ACCOUNTING FIRM
</B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">To
the Directors and Shareholders of FPU:</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
have audited the accompanying consolidated balance sheets and statements of
capitalization of Florida Public Utilities Company and its wholly-owned
subsidiary, Flo-Gas Corporation as of December 31, 2007 and 2006 and the related
consolidated statements of income, comprehensive income, common shareholders'
equity and cash flows for each of the three years in the period ended December
31, 2007. &nbsp;These financial statements are the responsibility of the
Company's management. &nbsp;Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these
financial statements based on our audits.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">We
conducted our audits in accordance with the standards of the Public Company
Accounting Oversight Board (United States). &nbsp;Those standards require that
we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the
financial statements are free of material misstatement. &nbsp;The Company is not
required to have, nor were we engaged to perform, an audit of its internal
control over financial reporting. Our audits included consideration of internal
control over financial reporting as a basis for designing audit procedures that
are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an
opinion on the effectiveness of the Company&#146;s internal control over financial
reporting. Accordingly, we express no such opinion. An audit also includes
examining, on a test basis, evidence supporting the amounts and disclosures in
the financial statements and schedules, assessing the accounting principles used
and significant estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall
presentation of the financial statements. We believe that our audits provide a
reasonable basis for our opinion.</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present
fairly, in all material respects, the financial position of Florida Public
Utilities Company and its wholly-owned subsidiary, Flo-Gas Corporation at
December 31, 2007 and 2006, and the results of its operation and its cash flows
for each of the three years in the period ended December 31, 2007, in conformity
with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.
&nbsp;</P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">As
discussed in Note&nbsp;2 to the consolidated financial statements, effective
January&nbsp;1, 2007, the Company adopted Financial Accounting Standard Board
(FASB) Interpretation No.&nbsp;48 &#147;Accounting for Uncertainty in Income Taxes
(an interpretation of FASB Statement No.&nbsp;109)&#148; and FASB Staff Position
No.&nbsp;FIN&nbsp;48-1 &#147;Definition of Settlement in FASB Interpretation
No.&nbsp;48.&#148; </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>

<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 0px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: -17px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>Item 9A.</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; TEXT-INDENT: 84px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"
align=justify><B>Controls and Procedures </B></P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px" align=justify><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Disclosure
Controls and Procedures</B></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 5px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">In
accordance with Exchange Act Rules&nbsp;13a-15 and 15d-15, we carried out an
evaluation, under the supervision and with the participation of management,
including our CEO and CFO, of the effectiveness of our disclosure controls and
procedures as of December&nbsp;31, 2007. Based on evaluation, our CEO and CFO
have concluded that, as of December&nbsp;31, 2007, our disclosure controls and
procedures were effective in that they provide reasonable assurance that
information required to be disclosed by us in our reports filed or submitted
under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (the &#147;Exchange Act&#148;) is recorded,
processed, summarized and reported within the time periods specified in the
SEC&#146;s rules and forms. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Management&#146;s
annual report on internal control over financial reporting</B></P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 11px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Management
of the Company is responsible for establishing and maintaining adequate internal
control over financial reporting as defined in Rules&nbsp;13a-15(f) and
15d-15(f) under the Exchange Act. The Company&#146;s internal control over financial
reporting is designed to provide reasonable assurance regarding the reliability
of financial reporting and the preparation of financial statements for external
purposes in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles. Because of
the inherent limitations, internal control over financial reporting may not
prevent or detect misstatements. Also, projections of any evaluation of
effectiveness to future periods are subject to the risk that controls may become
inadequate because of changes in conditions, or that the degree of compliance
with the policies or procedures may deteriorate. </P>
<P
style="MARGIN-TOP: 11px; FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">Management
assessed the effectiveness of the Company&#146;s internal control over financial
reporting as of December&nbsp;31, 2007. In making this assessment, management
used the criteria set forth by the Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of the
Treadway Commission in Internal Control-Integrated Framework. Based on such
assessment and those criteria, management believes that the Company&#146;s internal
control over financial reporting was effective as of December&nbsp;31, 2007.
</P>
<P style="MARGIN-TOP: 11px; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook"><B>Changes
in Internal Control Over Financial Reporting</B></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">There
have not been any changes in the Company&#146;s internal control over financial
reporting during the fiscal quarter ended December&nbsp;31, 2007 that have
materially affected, or are reasonably likely to materially affect, the
Company&#146;s internal control over financial reporting. </P>
<P style="MARGIN: 0px"><BR></P>
<P
style="FONT-SIZE: 11pt; MARGIN: 0px; LINE-HEIGHT: 13pt; FONT-FAMILY: Century Schoolbook">This
annual report does not include an attestation report of the Company&#146;s registered
public accounting firm regarding internal control over financial reporting.
Management&#146;s report was not subject to attestation by the Company&#146;s registered
public accounting firm pursuant to temporary rules&nbsp;of the SEC that permit
the Company to provide only management&#146;s report in this annual report.</P>
